, then we have

\eta(\phi)(\gamma_{0}\cdot x)(\gamma)=\phi(\gamma^{-1}\gamma_{0}x)=\eta(\phi)(x)(\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma)=(\gamma_{0}\cdot\eta(\phi)(x))(\gamma).

We define now an inverse map
{\rho}\colon{\Mor_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{F},\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\mathcal{G}))}\longrightarrow{\Mor_{\Gamma_{1}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})}

via
\rho(\psi)(x)\mathrel{:=}\psi(x)(1_{\Gamma})
. To see that this is well defined, we compute

\rho(\psi)(\gamma_{1}\cdot x)=\psi(\gamma_{1}\cdot x)(1_{\Gamma})=(\gamma_{1}\cdot\psi(x))(1_{\Gamma})=\psi(x)(\gamma_{1}^{-1})=\gamma_{1}\cdot\psi(x)(1_{\Gamma})=\gamma_{1}\cdot\rho(\psi)(x).

It is straightforward to check that
\eta
and
\rho
are mutually inverse, as desired.
∎

Lemma A.20
.

There is a natural isomorphism between the functors

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))^{\Gamma}
and
(\cdot)^{\Gamma_{1}}

from
{\Gamma_{1}}\text{-}{\Sh}_{\mathcal{C}}
to
\Sh_{\mathcal{C}}
,
given by evaluation at
1_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.
First, this is actually well-defined: let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf with
\Gamma_{1}
-action and let

{\phi}\colon{\Gamma}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}
be a
\Gamma_{1}
-equivariant morphism which is fixed by the

\Gamma
-action. If
{i}\colon{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma}
is the inclusion, then for all local sections

\gamma
of
\Gamma
and
\gamma_{1}
of
\Gamma_{1}
we have

\gamma_{1}\cdot\phi(1_{\Gamma})=\phi(\gamma_{1})=(i(\gamma_{1})^{-1}\cdot\phi)(1_{\Gamma})=\phi(1_{\Gamma}).

We now define the inverse natural transformation

{\eta}\colon{(\cdot)^{\Gamma_{1}}}\longrightarrow{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))^{\Gamma}}
as
follows: let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf with
\Gamma_{1}
-action and let
a
be a local section of

\mathcal{F}
which is fixed by the
\Gamma_{1}
-action. We define

{\eta(a)}\colon{\Gamma}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}
via
\eta(a)(\gamma)=a
for all
\gamma
, and note that
\eta
is clearly an inverse.
∎

Lemma A.21
.

Suppose that the quotient
\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}
is isomorphic to the constant sheaf

\underline{S}
for some set
S
and there is a sheaf-theoretic section

{\sigma}\colon{\underline{S}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma}
. There is a unique natural isomorphism

{\epsilon_{\sigma}}\colon{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))}\longrightarrow{\prod_{S}(\cdot)}

whose composition with the projection on the factor
corresponding to
s\in S
is given by evaluation at
\sigma(s)
.

Proof.
There is clearly a natural transformation as claimed. In the reverse direction, if
\mathcal{F}
is a

\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf and
(x_{s})_{s\in S}
is a local section of
\prod_{S}\mathcal{F}
, then we define
\eta_{\sigma}((x_{s})_{s\in S})(\sigma(s)\gamma_{1}^{-1})=\gamma_{1}\cdot x_{s}
, and check that
\epsilon_{\sigma}
and
\eta_{\sigma}
are mutually
inverse.
∎

We finally specialize to the category of finite type affine group schemes
over a ring (eventually, a field).
Thus, from now on, we fix a ring
k
and consider only the site
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{AffSch}_{k}
equipped with the fppf topology.
Our work in the sequel will require
some basic elements of descent theory, summarized in
Theorem
A.22
.
We will assume further after the theorem that

k
is a field, but we do not do so yet.

Theorem A.22
.

Let
k
be a ring, and let
k^{\prime}
be a faithfully flat
k
-algebra. Let
\mathcal{F}
be an fppf sheaf
over
k
, and suppose that the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{k^{\prime}}
to the category of affine
k^{\prime}
-schemes is
isomorphic to
h_{X^{\prime}}
for some affine
k^{\prime}
-scheme
X^{\prime}
. Then there is an affine
k
-scheme
X
such that
\mathcal{F}\cong h_{X}
. If
X^{\prime}
is of finite type (respectively, smooth), then the same is true of
X
.

Proof.
Let
{p_{1},p_{2}}\colon{\Spec(k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})}\longrightarrow{\Spec(k^{\prime})}
be the two projection maps. Note
that there is a natural isomorphism
p_{1}^{*}X^{\prime}\cong p_{2}^{*}X^{\prime}
of affine (
k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
)-schemes
between the pullbacks of
X^{\prime}
along
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
,
coming from the fact that the pullbacks

p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k^{\prime}}
both equal
\mathcal{F}_{k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}}
for
i=1,2
. This isomorphism is compatible
in the natural way with the three projection maps

{p_{ij}}\colon{\Spec(k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})}\longrightarrow{\Spec(k^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})}
, i.e., it forms
a
descent datum
in the sense of

[
bosch-lutkebohmert-raynaud:neron
]
*Section 6.1.
By
[
bosch-lutkebohmert-raynaud:neron
]
*6.1, Theorem 6,
it follows that there is an affine

k
-scheme
X
such that
\mathcal{F}\cong h_{X}
. The final claims follow from

[
grothendieck:EGA-IV.2
]
*Proposition 2.7.1

[
grothendieck:EGA-IV.4
]
*Corollaire 17.7.3
.
∎

We now assume that
k
is a field
(in addition to the standing assumption from before
Theorem
A.22
that

\mathcal{C}
is the site

\mathrm{AffSch}_{k}
).
It is now convenient for us to view
affine
k
-schemes as special sorts of fppf sheaves;
that is, we will leave the Yoneda embedding implicit,
so that, for example, we may ask whether
a sheaf
\mathcal{F}

is
a scheme, meaning that
it is of the form
h_{X}
for some scheme
X
.
In particular, we will now usually use the letter
X

and related notation,
rather than
\mathcal{F}
, for sheaves
when we expect most of the applications to be to schemes.

With this implicit identification in mind, we require that
the fppf group sheaf
\Gamma
is actually
a smooth finite type
k
-group scheme, and
the subgroup sheaf
\Gamma_{1}
is
an open
k
-subgroup scheme.
A
\Gamma_{1}
-scheme
is
a
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf that is a scheme, and
similarly for
\Gamma
-schemes.

Remark A.23
.

Let
{\widetilde{X}}_{1}
be an fppf
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf.
With the notation and hypotheses of
Lemma
A.21
,
we may equip
{\widetilde{X}}\mathrel{:=}\prod_{S}{\widetilde{X}}_{1}
with
an action of
\Gamma(k)
as follows.
For each
s\in S
, write
\pi_{s}
for the
corresponding projection
{{\widetilde{X}}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}
.
We equip each
\gamma\in\Gamma(k)
with
the unique action on
{\widetilde{X}}
so that

\pi_{s}\circ\gamma
equals

(\gamma s)^{-1}\sigma(\gamma s)\circ\pi_{\sigma(\gamma s)}

for every
s\in S
.
Then the morphism

\epsilon_{\sigma}
of
Lemma
A.21
is

\Gamma(k)
-equivariant.

Remark A.24
.

Let
A
be a
k
-algebra.

For every fppf
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf
{\widetilde{X}}_{1}
,
the fppf
\Gamma_{A}
-sheaves

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1\,A}}(\Gamma_{A},{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,A})
and

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})_{A}

are equal (not just naturally isomorphic!).

The functors

\WRes_{A/k}\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1\,A}}(\Gamma_{A},(\cdot))

and

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\WRes_{A/k}(\cdot))

from fppf
\Gamma_{1\,A}
-sheaves to fppf
\Gamma
-sheaves are
naturally isomorphic, because they are both left adjoint to
the “forgetful base-change” functor that sends
an fppf
\Gamma
-sheaf
{\widetilde{X}}
to

{\widetilde{X}}_{A}
, regarded as an fppf
\Gamma_{1\,A}
-sheaf.

Lemma A.25
.

There exists a finite separable extension
k^{\prime}/k
such that

(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}
is constant over
\Spec k^{\prime}
and the map

{\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}}
admits a section.

Proof.
Since
\Gamma
is smooth,
\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
is dense in
\Gamma_{{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}
. Thus there is a finite
separable extension
k^{\prime}
of
k
such that each component of
\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}
contains a
k^{\prime}
-point.
The conclusions of the lemma hold for this choice of
k^{\prime}
.
∎

Corollary A.26
.

The functor

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))
on fppf group
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaves over
k
is exact.

Proof.
Exactness of a sequence of group sheaves can be
checked after passage to a finite separable extension of

k
, so by Lemma
A.25
we may and do
assume that
\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}\cong\underline{S}
is
constant and the map
{\Gamma}\longrightarrow{\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}}
admits a
sheaf-theoretic section. In particular, if

1\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}\to 1
is an
exact sequence of fppf group sheaves over
k

equipped with
\Gamma_{1}
-actions, then Lemma
A.21

shows that the sequence obtained by
applying
\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))
can
be identified with the product sequence

1\to\prod_{s\in S}\mathcal{F}\to\prod_{s\in S}\mathcal{G}\to\prod_{s\in S}\mathcal{H}\to 1,

which is exact because finite products are exact in the category of sheaves.
∎

Write
k_{2}
for the dual numbers

k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})
.
The evaluation map that sends
\epsilon
to
0
provides
a ring homomorphism

{k_{2}}\longrightarrow{k}
, which we use to regard
k
as a
k_{2}
-algebra.
For any fppf
k
-sheaf
X
,
the identity map on
X=(X_{k_{2}})_{k}
provides a morphism

{X_{k_{2}}}\longrightarrow{\WRes_{k/k_{2}}X}
, and we obtain
by functoriality a canonical map

{\WRes_{k_{2}/k}X_{k_{2}}}\longrightarrow{\WRes_{k_{2}/k}\WRes_{k/k_{2}}X\cong X}
.
If
X
is a group sheaf, then, by definition, its Lie algebra is
the kernel of this map.

Corollary A.27
.

If
{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
is an fppf group
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf, then
the sheaf isomorphism

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\WRes_{k_{2}/k}{\widetilde{G}}_{1\,k_{2}})}\longrightarrow{\WRes_{k_{2}/k}\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1\,k_{2}}}(\Gamma_{k_{2}},{\widetilde{G}}_{1\,k_{2}})}

from Remark
A.24

restricts to a Lie-algebra isomorphism of

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1}))

onto

\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))
.

Proof.
It is clear that the sheaf isomorphism is also a group isomorphism.
That it carries

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1}))
onto

\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))
follows from
exactness of
\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))

(Corollary
A.26
), applied to
the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\WRes_{k_{2}/k}{\widetilde{G}}_{1\,k_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
.
Since the Lie-algebra structure on

\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1})
is deduced from
the group-sheaf structure on
{\widetilde{G}}_{1\,k_{2}}

[
demazure-gabriel:groupes-algebriques
]
*
Ch. II, §4, Proposition 4.5,
and analogously for

\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))
,
it follows that the restriction is
a Lie-algebra isomorphism.
∎

Lemma A.28
.

Write
p
for the characteristic exponent of
k
,

(\cdot)^{(p)}
for the Frobenius twist,
and
\operatorname{Frob}_{(\cdot)}

for the Frobenius natural transformation

{(\cdot)}\longrightarrow{(\cdot)^{(p)}}

[
demazure-gabriel:groupes-algebriques
]
*
Ch. II, §7, 1.1
(taken to be trivial if
p
equals
1
).
For every
\Gamma_{1}
-scheme
{\widetilde{X}}_{1}
,
we have that the subfunctors

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{(p)}
,

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})
, and

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})
of

\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{(p)}=\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})

are equal,
the functorial morphism

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}\longrightarrow{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}

is an isomorphism, and
the diagram

\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}\circ(\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\Frob}
\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\cdot)\circ\Frob_{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}

commutes.

Proof.
We have that

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{(p)}=(\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{\Gamma_{1}})^{(p)}

equals

(\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})^{(p)})^{\Gamma_{1}}=(\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)}))^{\Gamma_{1}}=\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})
.
Since

\Gamma_{1}
acts on

\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})

via

{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}
, which is a quotient map,
we have that

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})=\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})^{\Gamma_{1}}
equals

\underline{\Mor}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})^{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}=\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{\Gamma_{1}}}
\textstyle{\Gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}}}
\textstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Since the top row is exact,
the left and middle arrows are quotient maps, and
the right arrow is an isomorphism,
it follows from the nine lemma
and the fact that

\ker(\Frob_{\Gamma_{1}})
equals
\ker(\Frob_{\Gamma})

that the bottom row is exact.
That is, the functorial map

{\Gamma^{(p)}}\longrightarrow{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})^{(p)}}

factors through an isomorphism

{\Gamma^{(p)}/\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})^{(p)}}
.

To show that

{\Frob_{\Gamma}}\colon{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}\longrightarrow{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}

is an isomorphism,
it suffices to show that it becomes one after fppf base change.
Thus, by Lemma
A.25
, we may, and do,
assume that
\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}
is constant, and that
there is a section

{\sigma}\colon{\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma}
.
The composition of
the isomorphism

{\Gamma^{(p)}/\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})^{(p)}}

with

{\sigma^{(p)}}\colon{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})^{(p)}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma^{(p)}}

is a section of the quotient map

{\Gamma^{(p)}}\longrightarrow{\Gamma^{(p)}/\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}
.
Thus Lemma
A.21
shows that
the choice of
\sigma
furnishes isomorphisms

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)}}

and

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})=\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}^{(p)}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\prod_{(\Gamma^{(p)}/\Gamma_{1}^{(p)})(k)}{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)}}

such that

\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma^{(p)},{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\cdot)\circ\Frob_{\Gamma}}
\textstyle{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{(\Gamma^{(p)}/\Gamma_{1}^{(p)})(k)}{\widetilde{X}}_{1}^{(p)}}

commutes, where
the right-hand arrow comes from the identification of
the two indexing sets
via

\Frob_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{1}}
.
The result follows.
∎

Proposition A.29
.

If
{\widetilde{X}}_{1}
is an affine scheme over
k
with
\Gamma_{1}
-action, then

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})
is an affine
k
-scheme as well.
The properties
“of finite type”,
“smooth”,
“étale”,
and
“geometrically connected”, and,
when restricted to linear algebraic
k
-group schemes,
“connected” and
“of multiplicative type”,
are preserved by
\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,(\cdot))
.

Proof.
By Lemma
A.25
, there is a finite separable extension
k^{\prime}
of
k

such that
(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}
is finite and constant and
the map
{\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}}
admits a sheaf-theoretic section. By
Lemma
A.21
, the sheaf
\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})_{k^{\prime}}
is
(representable by) a finite power of
{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k^{\prime}}
and thus is an affine scheme over
k
.
By Theorem
A.22
, the functor
\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})
is therefore
representable by some affine
k
-scheme,
which is of finite type, respectively smooth, if

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k^{\prime}}
is.
Since all of the properties claimed to be preserved
can be checked after passing to a field extension
(see

[
grothendieck:EGA-IV.4
]
*Corollaire 17.7.3
and

[
milne:algebraic-groups
]
*A.59 and Definition 12.17),
the result follows.
∎

Remark A.30
.

The proof of Proposition
A.29
shows,
with the notation there, that

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})
is
a scheme by an abstract descent argument, as in
Theorem
A.22
.
We discuss how to realise this descent concretely in our case.
For each
\gamma\in\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
, we can define

k_{\gamma}
to be the fixed field in
{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
of

\stab_{\Gal(k)}(\gamma\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}}))
, and then construct
a
\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
-valued cocycle
c_{\gamma}
on
\Gal(k_{\gamma})
by

{\sigma}\longmapsto{\gamma^{-1}\sigma(\gamma)}
.
Then
\gamma^{-1}
provides a morphism
from the base-changed scheme
{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}}
to
its twist
{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}\,c_{\gamma}}
by
c_{\gamma}
, hence
a morphism

{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{\WRes_{k_{\gamma}/k}{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}\,c_{\gamma}}}
,
where
\WRes_{k_{\gamma}/k}
is the Weil restriction.
Put

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}=\WRes_{k_{\gamma}/k}{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}\,c_{\gamma}}
,
and
let us abuse notation by writing again
\gamma^{-1}
for
the map
{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}}
constructed above.

For every
\gamma_{1}\in\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
,
we have that

k_{\gamma\gamma_{1}}
equals
k_{\gamma}
,

c_{\gamma\gamma_{1}}
is cohomologous to
c_{\gamma}
,
so

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}\,c_{\gamma}}
is isomorphic to

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma\gamma_{1}}\,c_{\gamma\gamma_{1}}}
, and
there is
an isomorphism
{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma\gamma_{1}}}
such that
the diagram

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma\gamma_{1}^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma\gamma_{1}}}

commutes. Thus we may, and do, regard not just

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}
, but also
the map
{\gamma^{-1}}\colon{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}}
, as
depending only on
the coset
\gamma\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
Similarly, replacing
\gamma
by a
\Gal(k)
-conjugate replaces

k_{\gamma}
and

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,k_{\gamma}\,c_{\gamma}}

by the corresponding
\Gal(k)
-conjugates, but
does not change the isomorphism type of

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}
, affording a commutative diagram as above.
Thus we may, and do, regard both
{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}
and

{\gamma^{-1}}\colon{{\widetilde{X}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}}
as depending only on
the
\Gal(k)
-orbit of
\gamma\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
(So what we are really doing is considering, not just

{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}
, but the injective limit

\injlim_{(\sigma,\gamma_{1})\in\Gal(k)\ltimes\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})}\sigma({\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma\gamma_{1}})
.)
Now consider the map

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{X}}_{1})}\longrightarrow{\prod_{\gamma}{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,\gamma}},

where
\gamma
ranges over
the set of
\Gal(k)
-orbits on
\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})/\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
This map is an isomorphism, because,
upon base change to
{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
, it becomes

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k^{\textup{{s}}}},{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\longrightarrow{\prod_{\gamma\in\Gal(k)\backslash\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})/\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})}\prod_{\sigma\in\Gal(k)/\stab\gamma\Gamma_{1}(k)}{\widetilde{X}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}};

and this latter is an isomorphism by
Lemma
A.21

(with
S
there being
\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})/\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
).

Remark A.31
.

Let
p
be the characteristic exponent of
k
.
Write
[p]
for the trivial map, or
the
p
th-power map, on
the Lie algebra of a
k
-group scheme

[
demazure-gabriel:groupes-algebriques
]
*
Ch. II, §7, Proposition 3.4,
according as
p
does or does not equal
1
.
If
{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
is an affine group
\Gamma_{1}
-scheme and
we identify

\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))

with

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1}))

via

Corollary
A.27
, then
it follows from functoriality

[
demazure-gabriel:groupes-algebriques
]
*
Ch. II, §7, 1.1
that

\textstyle{\Gamma\times\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\times[p]}
\textstyle{\Gamma\times\underline{\Lie}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[p]}
\textstyle{\underline{\Lie}({\widetilde{G}}_{1})}

commutes, where the vertical maps are the evaluation maps.

Definition A.32
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}
is
an open subgroup scheme of the finite type
k
-group scheme
\Gamma
,
it is an open and closed subscheme of
\Gamma
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is a
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf, write
\iota_{\mathcal{F}}

for the natural transformation

{\mathcal{F}}\longrightarrow{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\mathcal{F})}

coming from the identification of

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma_{1},\mathcal{F})
with

\mathcal{F}
by evaluation at the identity, followed by
“extension by zero” to the complementary subscheme.

For a ring
A
and a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\in\Spec A
, let
k(\mathfrak{p})
be the residue field
of
A
at
\mathfrak{p}
, i.e.,
k(\mathfrak{p})=\operatorname{Frac}(A/\mathfrak{p})
. If
A
is a

k
-algebra and
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A)
, then we will say that
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}

lie in different
\Gamma_{1}
-cosets
if, for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\Spec A
, the elements

\gamma_{k(\mathfrak{p})^{\textup{{a}}}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{k(\mathfrak{p})^{\textup{{a}}}}
lie in different cosets for the
right translation action of
\Gamma_{1}(k(\mathfrak{p})^{\textup{{a}}})
on
\Gamma(k(\mathfrak{p})^{\textup{{a}}})
, where

k(\mathfrak{p})^{\textup{{a}}}
is an algebraic closure of
k(\mathfrak{p})
.

Proposition A.33
.

Let

{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
be a group
\Gamma_{1}
-sheaf, and

{\widetilde{H}}
a group
\Gamma
-sheaf.

(a)

The natural transformation

{\alpha}\colon{\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}})}\longrightarrow{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))}

given by

{\ell}\longmapsto{\bigl({\gamma}\longmapsto{\bigl({{\tilde{g}}_{1}}\longmapsto{\ell(\gamma^{-1}\cdot(\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}({\tilde{g}}_{1})))}\bigr)}\bigr)}
,
where
\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}
is as in Definition
A.32
,
is a monomorphism, natural in both
{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
and
{\widetilde{H}}
.

(b)

For every
k
-algebra
A^{\prime}
, the set of
A^{\prime}
-points of the
sheaf image

\alpha(\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}}))

consists of those

\phi\in\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))(A^{\prime})

such that, for every
A^{\prime}
-algebra
A
,
the subsets

\phi_{A}(\gamma)({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))
and

\phi_{A}(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))
of

{\widetilde{H}}(A)
commute whenever

\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A)
lie in
different
\Gamma_{1}(A)
-cosets.

Proof.
Let
k^{\prime}/k
be a finite separable extension of
k
such that

(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}
is constant and the map

{\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})_{k^{\prime}}}

admits a section
\sigma
, as we may by Lemma
A.25
.
In general, to check that a natural transformation of fppf sheaves over
k
is an isomorphism, it
suffices to pass to an fppf cover of
k
, so to
prove (
a
) we may and do pass from
k
to
k^{\prime}
. By
Lemma
A.21
, there is a natural isomorphism

{\epsilon_{\sigma}}\colon{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}

defined by
\epsilon_{\sigma}(\varphi)=(\varphi(\sigma(s)))_{s\in(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}
.
We have a similar isomorphism

{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}})}
.
Furthermore, the morphism
\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}
identifies with the inclusion into one factor of

\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
, so the first point is simply the statement that
a homomorphism
{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{H}}}
is
determined by the induced
(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)
-tuple of
homomorphisms
{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{H}}}
.

For (
b
), let
k^{\prime}/k
be as above.
Let
(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k^{\prime})=\{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}\}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is an fppf sheaf over
k
,
then the Weil restriction
\WRes_{k^{\prime}/k}(\mathcal{F})

(whose
A
-points are
\mathcal{F}(A\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})
)
is also an fppf sheaf.
Using this, we note that the proposed presheaf image
I_{\alpha}
of

\alpha
is already a sheaf: indeed, there is a map

{\Phi}\colon{\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}})}\longrightarrow{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\WRes_{k^{\prime}/k}(\SubSh({\widetilde{H}}_{k^{\prime}}))^{2}}

given by

{\phi}\longmapsto{\left(\im(\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma(\gamma_{i}))),C_{{\widetilde{H}}_{k^{\prime}}}(\im(\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma(\gamma_{i}))))\right)_{i}}.

The subpresheaf of

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\WRes_{k^{\prime}/k}(\SubSh({\widetilde{H}}_{k^{\prime}}))^{2}

consisting of those
(\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{G}_{n},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{n})

such that
\mathcal{G}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{j}
for all
i\neq j

is a sheaf
by Lemma
A.14
, and
I_{\alpha}
is
its preimage under

\Phi
, since to check that
\phi\in\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))(A^{\prime})
lies in

I_{\alpha}(A^{\prime})
it is enough to check that for every
A^{\prime}\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
-algebra
A
and every

i\neq j
, the subsheaves
\im(\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma(\gamma_{i})))_{A^{\prime}}
and

\im(\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma(\gamma_{j})))_{A^{\prime}}
commute. Thus in particular
I_{\alpha}
is a sheaf.

It is straightforward to check that
\alpha
factors through
I_{\alpha}

and to check that
\alpha
is an isomorphism onto
I_{\alpha}
,
it suffices to pass
from
k
to
k^{\prime}
. In that case, we use
\sigma
to identify

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1})
with
\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
and

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))
with

\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}})
.
In this case,
\alpha
is identified with the map

{\underline{\Hom}(\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}{\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}})}\longrightarrow{\prod_{(\Gamma/\Gamma_{1})(k)}\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}})}

whose composition with projection on the factor corresponding to

\gamma_{i}
is

{\ell}\longmapsto{\bigl({{\tilde{g}}_{1}}\longmapsto{\ell(\gamma_{i}^{-1}\cdot(\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}({\tilde{g}}_{1})))}\bigr)}
.
Thus the result is clear.
∎

Corollary A.34
.

Suppose that

{\widetilde{G}}_{1}
is a smooth affine group
\Gamma_{1}
-scheme
over
k
and
{\widetilde{H}}
is a group
\Gamma
-scheme.
Let

\phi
be an element of

\Mor_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{G}}_{1},{\widetilde{H}}))

such that

\phi_{k^{\textup{{s}}}}(\gamma)({\widetilde{G}}_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}}))
and

\phi_{k^{\textup{{s}}}}(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}}))
commute whenever

\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
lie in
different cosets for
the right-translation action of
\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
Then
\phi
is the image under

\alpha
of
the unique element
\ell
of

\Hom(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}})

such that

(
*
)

\ell_{k^{\textup{{s}}}}({\tilde{f}})\quad\text{equals}\quad\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})/\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})}\phi(\gamma)({\tilde{f}}(\gamma))

for all

{\tilde{f}}\in\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1})({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.

Proof.
We use repeatedly that certain schemes are smooth, and that
the rational points of a smooth scheme valued in
a separably closed field are
Zariski dense.

For example, Proposition
A.29
shows that

\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1})
is a smooth scheme, so
(

∗

‣
A.34
)
does indeed determine a unique element of

\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}})({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
Since the proposed element is fixed by
\Gal(k)
,
it comes from an element of

\Hom(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}})
.
Thus, since
\alpha_{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
is a monomorphism, we may, and do,
assume, after replacing
k
by
{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
, that

k
is separably closed.

First fix
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(k)
in
different
\Gamma_{1}(k)
-cosets.
Then, since
\phi(\gamma)({\widetilde{G}}_{1})
and

\phi(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1})
are
the Zariski closures of

\phi(\gamma)({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(k))
and

\phi(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(k))
, they
commute.

Now discard the fixed element

\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(k)
, and fix only

\gamma\in\Gamma(k)
.
Since the complementary subscheme to
the open subscheme
\gamma\Gamma_{1}
of

\Gamma
is
smooth, its set

\Gamma(k)\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}(k)
of

k
-points is Zariski dense. Moreover,
the closed subscheme of
\Gamma\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}
whose

A
-points, for every
k
-algebra
A
, are given by

\{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A)\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}(A)\,|\,\mathopen{}\text{$\phi_{A}(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))$ commutes with
$\phi(\gamma)_{A}({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))$}\}

contains

\Gamma(k)\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}(k)
.
By smoothness, we have for every such
A
and every

\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A)\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}(A)
that

\phi(\gamma)_{A}({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))

commutes with

\phi_{A}(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))
.

Finally, discard the fixed element
\gamma\in\Gamma(k)

(as well as
\gamma^{\prime}
).
Since
\Gamma
is smooth and
the closed subscheme whose
A
-points,
for every
k
-algebra
A
, are given by

\{\gamma\in\Gamma(A)\,|\,\mathopen{}\text{$\phi_{A}(\gamma^{\prime})({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))$ commutes with
$\phi_{A}(\gamma)({\widetilde{G}}_{1}(A))$ for every
$\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(A)\smallsetminus\gamma\Gamma_{1}(A)$}\},

contains
\Gamma(k)
,
we have that the criterion in
Proposition
A.33
(
b
)
for belonging to the image of
\alpha

is satisfied.
It follows from that result that
there is some

\ell\in\Hom(\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1}),{\widetilde{H}})

such that

\alpha(\ell)
equals
\phi
.
Now fix
{\tilde{f}}\in\Mor_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{G}}_{1})
.
Since

{\tilde{f}}
and

\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma(k)/\Gamma_{1}(k)}\gamma^{-1}\cdot\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}({\tilde{f}}(\gamma))

agree on
\Gamma(k)
, they are equal.
Thus

\ell({\tilde{f}})
equals

\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma(k)/\Gamma_{1}(k)}\ell(\gamma^{-1}\cdot\iota_{{\widetilde{G}}_{1}}({\tilde{f}}(\gamma)))
,
which, by definition, equals

\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma(k)/\Gamma_{1}(k)}\alpha(\ell)(\gamma)({\tilde{f}}(\gamma))=\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma(k)/\Gamma_{1}(k)}\phi(\gamma)({\tilde{f}}(\gamma)).\qed

Remark A.35
.

Suppose that
{\widetilde{T}}_{1}
is a group of multiplicative type, i.e., that
there is a
{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gal(k)]
-module
{\widetilde{M}}_{1}
such that

{\widetilde{T}}_{1}(A)
equals
\Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gal(k)]}({\widetilde{M}}_{1},({k^{\textup{{s}}}}\otimes_{k}A)^{\times})

for every
k
-algebra
A
.
Then
{\widetilde{M}}_{1}
is
isomorphic, as a
{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gal(k)]
-module, to

\Hom({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}},\GL_{1,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})={\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})
.
Suppose moreover that

{\widetilde{T}}_{1}
is equipped with an action of
\Gamma_{1}
.

Put
{\widetilde{T}}=\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{T}}_{1})
.
Proposition
A.29
already shows that

{\widetilde{T}}
is a group of multiplicative type, but
we can say more.
Namely, Proposition
A.33
provides
a natural isomorphism

{\alpha}\colon{\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{T}},\GL_{1,k})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{T}}_{1},\GL_{1,k}))}
,
hence a map

{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{k^{\textup{{s}}}})=\Hom({\widetilde{T}}_{k^{\textup{{s}}}},\GL_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\Mor_{\Gamma_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k^{\textup{{s}}}},\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}},\GL_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}))}

on
{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
-points.
We describe the inverse of this map concretely.

First,
the (
\Gal(k)\ltimes\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
)-equivariant map

{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\longrightarrow{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{k^{\textup{{s}}}})}

coming from the co-unit

{{\widetilde{T}}=\underline{\Mor}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\Gamma,{\widetilde{T}}_{1})}\longrightarrow{{\widetilde{T}}_{1}}

of the adjunction in Lemma
A.19

extends uniquely to a
(
\Gal(k)\ltimes\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
)-equivariant map

{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\longrightarrow{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{k^{\textup{{s}}}})}
.
Now choose
a set
S

of representatives for
the cosets of
\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
in
\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})
.
Each element of
S
gives a map

{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\longrightarrow{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}
, and
these maps assemble to an isomorphism

{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\prod_{S}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}
.
We also have an evaluation morphism

{\Mor_{\Gamma_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k^{\textup{{s}}}},\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}},\GL_{1,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}))}\longrightarrow{\prod_{S}\Hom({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}},\GL_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}
.
These maps fit together into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Mor_{\Gamma_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k^{\textup{{s}}}},\underline{\Hom}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}},\GL_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\Gamma_{1}({k^{\textup{{s}}}})]}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{k^{\textup{{s}}}}),}
\textstyle{\prod_{S}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\widetilde{T}}_{1\,{k^{\textup{{s}}}}})}

and the composition across the top row is the promised inverse of
the map on
{k^{\textup{{s}}}}
-rational points coming from
\alpha
.
